Trump dismissed the report . <p> Trump @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Trump said in September a deal was close and would involve a US$500-million payment by Harvard . <p> Cambridge , Massachusetts-based Harvard did not immediately respond to a Reuters request for comment . <p> The government has settled its probes with some universities . Harvard sued the Trump administration last year and a judge later ruled the government had unlawfully terminated some grants for the university . <p> Protesters , including some Jewish groups , say the government wrongly equates criticism of Israel 's assault on Gaza and its occupation of Palestinian territories with antisemitism , and advocacy for Palestinian rights with support for extremism . <p> Several other Ivy League schools , including Columbia University and Brown University , have reached agreements with the administration and accepted certain government demands . @@134125251 <h> Famine is threatening more of war-torn Sudan 's Darfur region as an attack in the south kills 22 <p> CAIRO ( AP ) -- Famine is threatening more areas in war-torn Sudan 's western Darfur region , a global hunger monitoring group said Thursday as an attack by paramilitary forces on a military hospital in the country 's south killed 22 people , including the hospital 's director and three members of its medical staff . <p> Since April 2023 , Sudan has been in the throes of war after a power struggle erupted between the military and the powerful paramilitary Rapid Support Forces , or RSF . The conflict has triggered what the United Nations calls the world 's worst humanitarian crisis . <p> The Integrated Food Security Phase Classification , or IPC , released a new report saying that acute malnutrition has reached famine levels in two more towns in Darfur . It stopped short of confirming a full famine in the towns . <p> The attack Thursday in the town of Kouik in South Kordofan province , also left eight people wounded , the Sudan Doctors @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ war said . It was not immediately clear how many of the casualties were civilians . <p> The attack was " not an isolated incident , but rather part of a series of attacks that have plagued South Kordofan , " the network said , adding that the assaults have left " several hospitals inoperable . " <p> The U.N . estimates that over 40,000 people have been killed in the war in Sudan , but aid agencies consider that the true number could be many times higher . Over 14 million people have been forced to flee their homes . <p> A harrowing report <p> The IPC report said famine-level malnutrition has been registered in the towns of Umm Baru and Kernoi in North Darfur province . In November , the group said that along with el-Fasher , the city of Kadugli in South Kordofan was also enduring famine . At the time , it also said 20 other areas across Sudan were at risk of famine . <p> In Umm Baru , nearly 53% of children between aged between 6 months and nearly 5 years suffered from acute @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ threshold , which stands at 30% . In Kernoi , 32% of children are suffering from malnutrition , the group said . <p> " These alarming rates suggest an increased risk of excess mortality and raise concern that nearby areas may be experiencing similar catastrophic conditions , " the report said . <p> Since the eruption of Sudan 's civil war , the IPC has confirmed famine in a total of seven areas . The group said it could not confirm a full famine in Umm Baru and Kernoi as access and lack of data makes it difficult to confirm the other two thresholds -- access to food and mortality -- that need to be reached for a famine to be confirmed . <p> The fall of el-Fasher in October 2025 to the RSF set off an exodus of people to nearby towns , straining the resources of neighboring communities and driving up food insecurity rates , the report said . <p> The IPC has confirmed famine only a few times , most recently in 2025 in northern Gaza during the Israel-Hamas war . It also confirmed famine in Somalia @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 2020 . <p> In 2024 , famine had struck five other areas in North Darfur and also Sudan 's Nuba Mountains region . <p> The IPC report also warned that more people might face extreme hunger in Kordofan , where the conflict has disrupted food production and supply lines in besieged towns and isolated areas . <p> " An immediate and sustained ceasefire is critical to avert further destitution , starvation , and death in the affected parts of Sudan , " pled the Rome-based group . <p> According to experts , famine is determined in areas where deaths from malnutrition-related causes reach at least two people , or four children under 5 years of age , per 10,000 people ; at least one in five people or households severely lack food and face starvation ; and at least 30% of children under age 5 suffer from acute malnutrition based on a weight-to-height measurement -- or 15% based on upper-arm circumference . <p> Fighting rages on <p> Since the RSF overran el-Fasher , which had been one of the army 's last strongholds in Darfur , fighting has recently concentrated @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ military began making gains in Kordofan after breaking a siege in Kadugli and the neighboring town of Dilling . <p> On Tuesday , the Sudanese military announced that it had opened a crucial road between Dilling and Kadugli , which had been under siege by the RSF since the start of the war . The RSF launched a drone attack Tuesday that hit a medical center in Kadugli , killing 15 people including seven children , according to Sudan Doctors Network . <p> Also this week , the United States and the U.N . said they are seeking to rally international support for humanitarian aid to Sudan , kicking off a new Sudan Humanitarian Fund with $700 million in contributions from the United Arab Emirates and the U.S. <p> The Trump administration said Tuesday it would contribute $200 million to the initiative from a basket of $2 billion it set aside late last year to fund humanitarian projects around the world . The UAE said it would contribute $500 million . Saudi Arabia and several other participants promised they would make pledges but did not specify amounts . <p> This @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ group did not confirm a full famine in the Darfur towns of Umm Baru and Kernoi but said that malnutrition there had reached famine-levels.

Follow her on Bluesky : **25;1505;TOOLONG <p> It was invigorating to see Prime Minister Mark Carney stand up to Donald Trump in Davos , even though Carney 's pithy speech -- and the adulation it won in European capitals -- left the U.S. president seething over sharing the spotlight with a mere central banker who 'd never had to prove himself on reality TV . <p> Carney 's Davos speech was a clarion call for " middle powers " to join Canada in resisting the U.S. hegemon as it fully abandons the international rules-based order : " Middle powers must act together because if we 're not at the table , we 're on the menu . " <p> Carney is correct : we should act together to resist the lawless , power-mad @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ his enthusiasm for a middle-power alliance , Carney devoted little attention to the one forum that the world constructed -- amid the ruins of the Second World War -- specifically to deal with a ruthless megalomaniac with designs on other countries . <p> That is , of course , the United Nations , established when the world 's nations came together , in the aftermath of Hitler , to create a body to restrain aggressors and replace the ravages of war with peace and human development . <p> For all its flaws , the UN remains the embodiment -- and the closest humans have come -- to achieving those worthy aspirations . <p> It is the UN , and its International Court of Justice , that establish the rules-based international order to protect the sovereignty of all nations . <p> But the UN is only as strong as its members make it . <p> And in recent years , it 's been weakened by the Trump administration , which has deprived it of funding , sought to undermine its authority and now effectively seeks to replace it with the preposterous @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and headed by Trump himself . <p> . <p> So , it 's disappointing that Carney 's Davos speech barely mentioned the UN , and focused instead on an alliance of middle powers , leaving many of the world 's nations " on the menu . " <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> We saw this western-focused approach from Carney last month when he raised no objections to Trump 's kidnapping of Venezuelan President Nicolas Maduro , but was outspoken against Trump 's threats against Greenland , which is part of the kingdom of Denmark . <p> Surely , we want a rules-based international order that seeks to protect the rights and security of all the world 's nations -- as the UN does -- not just the western countries that would make up a coalition of middle powers . <p> Interestingly , Canada played a significant role at the UN in its early years , ensuring that middle and smaller powers had some real influence . Canada was also pivotal in developing UN peacekeeping . <p> Canada remains supportive of the UN , but we 've @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ robust UN champion . <p> In 2017 , for instance , the Trudeau government refused to join when two-thirds of UN member nations ratified an ambitious treaty aimed at ultimately ridding the world of nuclear weapons . <p> And Carney -- even as he rebuked Trump in Davos for rupturing international law -- had nothing to say about an egregious breach of international law that same day , as Israeli forces stormed and then burned down the Jerusalem offices of the UN aid agency UNRWA , on which millions of Palestinian depend for survival . <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> For that matter , Carney has been largely silent in recent months as Israel has blocked UN aid deliveries and continued to kill Palestinians -- more than 500 Palestinians have been killed since the Gaza " ceasefire " -- in what genocide scholars have called a " genocide . " <p> Carney is right that we can no longer rely on the U.S. to enforce international law . In fact , we never could . Gift this article to friends and family for free . <h> Columnist missed one key point <p> Re : Rosemary Ganley 's recent column . I have a great deal of respect for the care and clarity Rosemary so often brings to her column , and for the way she often engages difficult moral questions . <p> I have long valued her work , her lifelong commitment to justice , and her thoughtful commentary on issues . It is in that spirit of respect that I write with deep concern about what feels like a devastating omission in her article . <p> To speak about immorality in the current geopolitical moment without acknowledging the ongoing genocide in Gaza felt impossible to read without responding . <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> I know Rosemary cares deeply about this issue . At present , the forces working to mask this ongoing horror and divert attention from what is happening are relentless . Its mandate resembles a lucrative real estate venture built on greed and the exploitation of dispossessed Indigenous land , converting it into casinos and golf courses , creating a playground for the global elite while Palestinians serve as cheap labour . <p> By leaving this unspoken , by remaining silent here , we risk contributing to the erasure of one of the gravest and ongoing crimes of settler colonialism in our lifetime . Silence in this moment is not neutral . <h> We do n't have free-market capitalism <p> The writer asked a great question . The answer is that we do n't have " free-market " capitalism . Free-market capitalism is an idyllic form @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> What may answer to the writer 's question is that we actually have what is called state capitalism , where the economy is controlled by the government . This explains why corporations get the lowest tax rates and bailouts in tough times , although they have the capital to lobby our governments toward their interests rather than those of the people . <p> That 's why Doug Ford , whose interests are aligned with entrenched corporations , like domestic auto manufacturers , has taken issue with Chinese EVs . They 've also done an excellent job convincing us that we are in a free-market form of capitalism . <p> Casey Bloom , Peterborough <p> Opinion articles are based on the author 's interpretations and judgments of facts , data and events . The hilly frontier zone where the UNIFIL force patrols has @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Hezbollah militants fought a full-scale war in 2024 . <p> The targeting of peacekeepers appears aimed at undermining the international force and strengthening Israel 's military footprint along the U.N.-drawn border with Lebanon , known as the Blue Line , the report alleges . It was shared with AP on condition that the news organization not identify the country whose peacekeepers compiled the findings for internal use by their senior command . <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> Israel has long mistrusted UNIFIL , accusing it of failing to prevent Hezbollah from building up its military presence along the border in violation of ceasefire agreements going back two decades . <p> The growing catalog of run-ins comes as a half-century of international peacekeeping efforts along the border face an uncertain future . UNIFIL 's mission is scheduled to end this year and U.S. President Donald Trump ' s administration regards it as a waste of money . <p> Israel says it tries to reduce harm <p> In a statement to AP , the Israeli military said it " is not conducting a deterrence campaign against UNIFIL forces " and is working @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ southern Lebanon . <p> The military " takes steps to reduce harm to UNIFIL forces and other international actors operating in the area , " it said . <p> UNIFIL said in a statement that " the number of attacks on or near peacekeepers , as well as aggressive behavior toward peacekeepers , have increased since September 2025 , " with most of those incidents attributed to the Israeli military . <p> " The majority of incidents do not involve physical harm to peacekeepers , but any action that interferes with our mandated activities is a matter of concern , " it said . <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> The U.N . force has reported additional incidents this year . An Israeli tank opened fire with small-caliber bullets on a UNIFIL post on Jan. 16 , it said . In some cases , UNIFIL vehicles were damaged . <p> The last four months of 2025 also saw a surge in incidents of direct fire at all targets from Israeli positions on both sides of the Blue Line , the report says . Such incidents spiked to 77 in December , up from just two in January , it says . <p> UNIFIL vehicles and positions are clearly marked as belonging to the U.N. , and Hezbollah militants have not maintained a visible presence or fired on Israeli forces in recent months . <p> The report says " it can not be excluded " that Israel is using the incidents to maintain a military presence north of the border and prevent people who have fled the zone from returning . <p> Israel-Hezbollah conflict <p> After the Oct. 7 , 2023 , Hamas -led attack on Israel that triggered war in Gaza , Hezbollah began firing rockets from Lebanon into Israel in support of Hamas and the Palestinians . <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . The low-level conflict escalated into full-scale war in September 2024 , later reined in but not fully stopped by a U.S.-brokered ceasefire two months later . <p> Since then , Israel has accused Hezbollah of trying to rebuild in the south , in violation of the ceasefire , and has carried out near-daily strikes in Lebanon that it says target Hezbollah militants and facilities . Israeli forces also continue to occupy five hilltop points on the Lebanese side of the border . Hezbollah has claimed one strike against Israel since the ceasefire . <p> Spraying of chemicals spurs an outcry <p> The U.N . and Lebanon say Israeli forces dropped herbicide on Lebanese territory on Sunday , forcing a more than nine-hour pause in peacekeeping activities , including patrols . <p> " The use of herbicides raises questions about the effects on local agricultural lands , and how this might impact the return of civilians to their homes and livelihoods in the long-term , " U.N . spokesperson St ? phane Dujarric said . @@134174909 <h> Trump plans to hold first peace board meeting in Washington this month <p> U.S. President Donald Trump speaks during a session on his Board of Peace initiative at the Annual Meeting of the World Economic Forum in Davos , Switzerland on Jan 22 . Markus Schreiber/The Associated Press <p> U.S. President Donald Trump plans to convene the first meeting of his Board of Peace this month in Washington to raise money for the reconstruction of Gaza . <p> The meeting , proposed for Feb. 19 , would include both world leaders who accepted Trump 's invitation in January to join the board as well as members of an executive committee for Gaza that will oversee the specifics of the territory 's governance , security and redevelopment , two Trump administration officials said Saturday . <p> It was not immediately clear how many leaders would accept the Republican president 's invitation , according to the officials , who spoke on condition of anonymity because the meeting has not yet been formally announced and details of its agenda were still being determined . <p> One official said the administration expected " @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ that was sent late Friday to invited participants and obtained by The Associated Press , says the meeting will be held at the U.S. Institute of Peace , now known as the Donald J. Trump U.S. Institute of Peace , pending an ongoing legal battle with the former leadership of the nonprofit think tank . But it has taken shape with his ambition for a far broader mandate of resolving global crises and appears to be the latest U.S. effort to sidestep the United Nations as Trump aims to reset the post-World War II international order . <p> Many of America 's top allies in Europe and elsewhere have declined to join what they suspect may be an attempt to rival the Security Council .

CORTINA D'AMPEZZO , Italy ( AP ) -- Seeing if Lindsey Vonn can contend for a medal in the Olympic downhill at age 41 with a titanium replacement in her right knee to cap her comeback after nearly six years of retirement was already going to be one of the highlights of the Milan Cortina Olympics . <p> " This would be the best comeback I 've done so far , " Vonn said . " Definitely the most dramatic . " <p> So dramatic that it has motivated plenty of people to question whether it 's something out of science fiction or attention hunting . Which is just the type of motivation that Vonn thrives on . <p> Take Vonn 's response on social media to an opinion columnist . <p> " My life does not revolve around ski racing . I am a woman that loves to ski . I do n't have an identity issue , I know exactly who I am . I was retired for 6 years and I have an amazing life , " Vonn posted Saturday on X. " I do n't need to ski , but I love to ski . I came all this way for one final Olympics and I 'm going to go and do my best , ACL or no . It 's as simple as that . " <p> Sofia Goggia , Vonn 's biggest rival in downhill and one of her best friends on the circuit , recalled how she came back to win silver at the 2022 Beijing Games after a similarly severe injury . <p> Goggia had a few weeks to recover after spraining her left knee , partially tearing her ACL and suffering a minor fracture of the fibula bone in her leg , plus some tendon damage . Vonn is attempting to do this nine days after her crash in the final downhill before the Winter Games . <p> " I remember how I felt -- and I did n't tear it completely , " said Goggia , who lit the Olympic cauldron in Cortina on Friday . The Italian then used a Latin phrase , " Ai posteri l'ardua sentenza , " to suggest that Vonn 's performance should only be judged by future generations . <p> Even Mikaela Shiffrin , the skier who broke Vonn 's all-time women 's World Cup wins record , ca n't wait to see what her fellow American can do in a race that starts at 11:30 a.m. in Cortina ( 5:30 a.m . ET ) . <p> " I am so excited to watch . I think we all are , " Shiffrin said upon arriving in Cortina , where she will start competing on Tuesday in the team combined , possibly pairing with Vonn in a skiing " dream team . " <p> Vonn 's race is drawing so much attention that the International Olympic Committee moved its daily media briefing on Sunday a half-hour earlier to avoid a conflict with the downhill . <p> Shiffrin said she appreciated Vonn 's " tenacity and grit . " <p> " I will be cheering and gripped to the TV , " Shiffrin added . " I have 100% belief that anything is possible . She 's done such incredible things despite injury , through injury , before . I have a lot of belief . " <p> Shiffrin also mentioned two other teammates who she 'll be cheering on -- and with good reason . Jacqueline Wiles and Breezy Johnson led the first and second downhill training sessions , respectively , and can also contend for medals . <p> Vonn holds the record of 12 World Cup wins in Cortina . Goggia , meanwhile , has four World Cup wins in Cortina and will have home crowd support . <p> Other contenders include Emma Aicher , the 22-year-old German who was n't even born when Vonn first started racing in Cortina ; Kajsa Vickhoff Lie , who was second in the opening training and carried Norway 's flag at the opening ceremony ; and Kira Weidle-Winkelmann , a German who has finished second in two downhills this season and was second in the final training . <p> As for the downhill course , the highlight is the Tofana schuss , a narrow chute between two walls of Dolomite rock where the skiers accelerate to 80 mph ( 130 kph ) . <p> But the real key to the Olympia delle Tofane track comes above the schuss , where there 's a key right turn that includes an uphill stretch . <p> " It 's incredibly reverse banked , " said Kristian Ghedina , the Cortina native and former racer who grew up in a home just below the finish line . " That 's where your speed for the rest of the course gets determined and if you do n't take the right trajectory it makes a huge difference because you end up going uphill . " <p> After the schuss , there 's a challenging left turn called Delta where the slope takes on a new downhill dimension <p> " It 's called Delta because that 's where hang gliders -- " deltaplano " is the word for a hang glider in Italian -- take off during the summer , " Ghedina said . <p> Aksel Lund Svindal , the 2018 Olympic downhill champion from Norway who now coaches Vonn , noted how a large brace that Vonn will be wearing over her left knee negatively affects her aerodynamics , but added , " We 're not focusing on that because if she starts to ask to take the brace away I think there 's a couple of doctors that would have something to say about that . Please do n't ask her that question . " <p> Actually , it 's probably better not to question Vonn at all . <p> She was also battered before the 2019 world championships but took bronze in downhill before going into a nearly six-year retirement . <p> " I 've been in this position before . I know how to handle it , " Vonn said . " I feel a lot better now than I did in 2019 . . . And I still got a medal there with no LCL and three tibial plateau fractures . So , like I said , this is not an unknown for me . I 've done this before . " <p> As evening arrived in Cortina , Vonn took to social media to note that she will race tomorrow in her final Olympic downhill " and while I ca n't guarantee a good result , I can guarantee I will give it everything I have . But no matter what happens , I have already won . " <p> " I will stand in the starting gate tomorrow and know I am strong . Know that I believe in myself . Know that the odds are stacked against me with my age , no ACL , and a titanium knee- but know that I still believe , " Vonn wrote on Instagram . " And usually , when the odds are stacked against me the most , I pull the best of what 's inside me out . " <p> BORMIO , Italy ( AP ) -- Swiss racer Franjo von Allmen brought home the first gold medal at the Milan Cortina Games by winning the downhill on Saturday . It 's his first Olympic crown in his very first Olympic race . <p> Not so long ago , it was n't clear if there would be anymore " firsts " for him in ski racing . As a teenager , and after the death of his father , von Allmen did n't have the money for racing -- until a crowdfunding project helped out . <p> Now , the 24-year-old is king of the downhill . <p> On a picture-perfect day in Bormio , von Allmen powered through the challenging Stelvio course in 1 minute , 51.61 seconds to keep the Olympic downhill title in the Swiss family . His time was good enough to withstand the charge of Italy 's Giovanni Franzoni , who finished .20 seconds back to claim the silver medal . Franzoni 's teammate , 36-year-old Dominik Paris , had a fast run to take bronze and knock Marco Odermatt of Switzerland off the podium . <p> " I had fun with the skiing today , " von Allmen said . <p> His skiing career was almost derailed at 17 . Through crowdfunding he raised money and qualified for the national C team . He showed his vast promise by winning three silver medals , including one in downhill , at the 2022 junior worlds in Canada . <p> That particular time , however , was not something he wanted to revisit after the race . <p> " For me that chapter is closed , " he said through a translator . " I prefer to concentrate on what 's yet to come and what 's happening now . " <p> Aksel Lund Svindal , the 2018 Olympic downhill champion from Norway , considered von Allmen a favorite . Svindal was impressed with the young racer 's performance during a downhill win in Switzerland leading into the Games . <p> " It 's the skiing of someone that 's really in form so I 'm not surprised , " Svindal said from Cortina , where he 's coaching Lindsey Vonn . " Very good skiing , obviously . " <p> The Swiss skier sometimes goes under the radar of his compatriot Odermatt , who 's won four straight overall World Cup titles . They 're fast friends and friendly rivals . They share course information but hold a little back , too . Turns out , von Allmen had the better inside track on the demanding hill . <p> " It 's really ( crap ) , to be fourth in the Olympics , " Odermatt said through a translation . " That 's the stupidest place that you can have . " <p> This has been quite a run for von Allmen , who won the downhill at the world championships in Austria last winter . He 's using the same ski technician that Swiss racer Beat Feuz relied on to win the downhill at the 2022 Beijing Games before retiring . <p> " It feels like a movie , " von Allmen said . " How could you imagine the Olympics better than to start with a gold medal ? Amazing . It 's difficult to describe it in words . " <p> Home favorites <p> The stands were packed with flag-waving fans . Some of the biggest cheers were reserved for Italy 's Franzoni and Paris , who went back-to-back . <p> This is a breakout season for the 24-year-old Franzoni , who 's from Manerba del Garda , located about 200 kilometers south of Bormio . More specifically , it 's been a breakout 22 or so days . He won a World Cup super-G in Wengen , Switzerland , on Jan. 16 and followed that up with a downhill victory in Kitzb ? hel , Austria , the following week . <p> To share a podium with Paris , well , that 's something Franzoni voiced into reality . " <p> Paris knows this course well , having won seven World Cup races at the venue . He now becomes a bronze medalist in addition to being the vocalist of a heavy metal band called " Rise of Voltage . " <p> " I 'm , for sure , a better skier , " Paris cracked . " It 's a really hard visual . <p> " I do n't know if we 've ever played on ice that straight . " <p> There was the usual five feet of curl on each sheet at the start of round-robin play on Wednesday . Rocks were textured -- also called sanding or papering -- on Friday night , as is standard around the midway point of an event . <p> The challenge for the icemaking crew can then be trying to find the right balance when scraping the four sheets before each session . Overclipping the frozen pebble on the ice surface can cause freshly sanded rocks to curl too much . <p> " We used caution and underclipped , " deputy chief ice technician Greg Ewasko said via email , adding the ice would be back to normal in the afternoon . <p> Gallant and Peterman extended their losing streak to three games in the evening when they dropped an 8-6 decision to Estonia 's Marie Kaldvee and Harri Lill . <p> The Canadians fell to 3-3 , a nervy position with only four of 10 teams making the playoff cut . <p> Great Britain topped Korey Dropkin and Cory Thiesse of the United States 6-4 in the afternoon to secure a playoff spot at 7-0 . Statistics from pre-game draws for the hammer are used to break ties for the playoffs if needed . <p> Peterman is all too familiar with that scenario . She missed the cut at the 2022 Beijing Games when her women 's team skipped by Jennifer Jones was 5-4 , but too far behind on LSD numbers . <p> " I watch some of the athletes who have one chance at a one-minute run for their entire Olympic career , " said Canadian coach Scott Pfeifer . " And out here we get to play nine games , and yes , there 's an LSD instead of a tiebreaker ( game ) . <p> " There 's plenty of opportunities to make it into the playoffs based on the virtue of your play on the ice . And that 's all we 're focusing on . " <p> Dodds made a hit to pick out a Canadian stone for the opening three-ender and made another hit for two points in the third . Canada used its power play in the fourth end but could only generate a single point . <p> Great Britain shot 83 per cent overall while Canada was well back at 74 per cent . <p> " They 're just great throwers of the rock , " Gallant said . Canada missed the mixed doubles playoffs in 2022 and won gold when the discipline made its Olympic debut in 2018 at Pyeongchang , South Korea . <h> How Curling Canada changed its ways ahead of Milan Olympics <p> Canadian curler Brett Gallant is watched by Curling Canada staff during a technical training session in Calgary . <p> Randy Risling/Special to the Star Toronto Star <p> A decade and a half ago , Curling Canada 's chief executive Nolan Thiessen played lead on the men 's team that won the world championship in Cortina d'Ampezzo , the Italian mountain town where curlers will vie for Olympic medals starting Wednesday . <p> Back then , each member played with a particular style : different starting positions , slides and releases . When someone 's in-turn curled more than their out-turn , they did n't try to fix that . They just put their brooms to work . <p> From Thiessen 's day until quite recently , Canadian curlers more or less did their own thing . That 's changed since Scottish import David Murdoch arrived in 2023 to lead Canada 's high-performance program and usher in a more modern era of technical training , video analysis and , crucially , getting everyone to deliver the rock the same way . <p> For those early risers watching Canada 's mixed doubles team compete against Great Britain this morning , here 's a relevant Star story from before the Games : <h> Ski jumping <p> In case you 're wondering what counts as a " normal " hill for ski jumpers , it 's 107 metres tall , about a 30-storey building , versus the " large " hill event at 141 metres , more like a 40-storey tower . Strate is usually stronger on the large hill -- and women have finally been given that second event at these Olympics -- but she 's had such strong World Cup results this season that she 's worth watching in any event . <h> Big air snowboarding <p> At 1:30 p.m . Canadian Francis ( Frank ) Jobin is competing in the snowboard big air final . At the last Olympics , Crawford won bronze in alpine combined and in downhill training on Friday he produced the fastest time . The event runs from 5:30-7:50 a.m . <p> The downhill races at the last three Winter Olympics in Russia , Korea and China were contested on new courses . Canada 's mixed doubles team of Jocelyn Peterman and Brett Gallant lost to Great Britain early this morning , dropping their record to 3-2 , and will meet Estonia at 1:05 p.m . <p> Read more <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> Updated 1 hr ago <h> Abigail Strate is Canada 's top ski jumping medal hope at the Olympics -- as long as the fear does n't creep in <p> Canada 's Abigail Strate celebrates winning her first career World Cup ski jumping event on New Year 's Day on the large hill in Oberstdorf , Germany . " I frickin ' did it . " <p> Karl-Josef Hildenbrand / AP file photo Toronto Star <p> MILAN -- Ski jumping gave us " the agony of defeat . " <p> To wit : Slovenia 's Vinko Bogataj going spectacularly arse over teakettle , limbs flailing , twisting and tumbling and crashing , a breathtaking wipeout that for decades was featured in the opening montage for ABC 's " Wide World of Sports . " <p> Bogataj actually came out of that wreckage mostly intact , mild concussion and fractured ankle . <p> Abigail Strate , Canada 's medal hopeful for the Milan Cortina Olympics , has got him beat on that triage front : two severe concussions a few months apart in 2024 , torn ACL , LCL and meniscus a couple of years before , tibial stress fracture in there somewhere . <p> " Head injuries are the ones that scare me the most . You can heal a knee , but if your mess up your head too many times ... " <p> It takes cojones to jump off a 110-metre hill with no brakes , body flinging through the air -- Strate has flown up to 141 metres in training -- then planting a solid Telemark landing . <p> " You have to be really brave to do this sport , " says Strate . " In training or a normal competition , I do n't have fear , especially when I 'm performing at my best . I really am able to shut that off , but sometimes it creeps in . Those really bad crashes last season , they really messed up my mental space . Actually I was pretty terrified to be ski jumping for a while . <p> " But that was in the middle of the World Cup season , so I just put my head down and kept going . " Twentieth-place finishes , far off her standard results , sent Strate to a mental-health coach . " I had to revisit the experience that caused that fear and find trust again in my ability and my training . " <p> It was an effective approach as Strate rebounded magnificently with two World Cup silvers in 2025 , two silvers on one weekend last month and , on New Year 's Day 2026 , her first career World Cup gold on the large hill in Oberstdorf , Germany . " I frickin ' did it , " the 24-year-old from Calgary crowed . <p> " To have it happen on the first day of the year was a pretty good start . " <p> She had , Strate said , " jumped from my soul . " <p> " That means getting away from results , " Strate told the Star in an interview before leaving Team Canada 's training base in Planica , Slovenia -- the country is mad for ski jumping and the Canadian team is coached by Slovenians -- and heading for an event in Japan , the final competition before the Games , where she copped that double silver . <p> " I tend to overthink a lot . Like , oh , if this does n't work out . When I 'm jumping really just for the sake of ski jumping , loving it , enjoying it , that 's when the best results happen . Not even thinking about winning . " <p> Mental discipline is required even before she steps onto the lip of the takeoff table : " For me , it 's really important how exactly I leave the bar . I get in position already right from there , focusing . If my mind is wandering to other things , like the noise or the weather , it 's already kind of over for me . So I 'm thinking of what I want to be feeling on the in-run , what I want to do on the takeoff . Pretty much trying to visualize the whole jump before I take it . <p> " There 's a fraction of a second where every part of your body , every little muscle , has to be going in the right place and working the right way . But it all happens so quick . There 's a lot of relying on muscle memory and being relaxed , which is really hard when you 're under the stress of competition , especially at something as high stakes as the Olympics . " <p> She 'll know instantly if it will be a good jump : " You can tell from the takeoff if it 's going to fly smooth and fly far . My adrenalin is pumping . Feeling what the skis are doing , what position my ankles are in , where my arms are . It 's all technical stuff because ski jumping is based on physics and aerodynamics . You can definitely feel when you 're doing the right things in the air and when you make a mistake . I need to be relaxed moving as little as I can in the flight . " <p> On the normal hill ( her first Milan Olympics event on Saturday ) Strate will be suspended in the air for about five seconds . On the big hill , about eight seconds . While ski jumping has been an Olympic event since 1924 , women have been permitted to compete only since 2014 -- inclusion a long , hard fight -- and will make their big hill debut at these Games . Ski flying , the extreme version of the sport ( Strate has only done it twice ) is done on significantly higher hills achieving speeds of more than 105 km/h , hanging air for upwards of 10 seconds . It 's not on the Olympic menu . <p> The thrill of ski jumping has never worn off for the Toronto Film School student , who 's also a certified beekeeper and design artist by trade . She took up the sport as a six-year-old . Even then , she was a speed demon : " Honestly , I do n't know what my parents were thinking . " <p> From then to now , self-confidence is the key : " That 's not something I was born with , unfortunately . It was something I had to figure out . " <p> Canada 's team is composed of only four jumpers . <p> " To be a Canadian ski jumper specifically , you have to be extremely stubborn and determined . It 's not easy to move across the world . I was out here already when I was 14 years old . I miss my family , but do n't hate the sport for taking me away from them . You 've got to have grit . It 's pretty miraculous that with the small group of people that we do have that we have so much of that grit . " <p> " We believed anything was possible , then we went and made the impossible possible . ' ' <p> The team has steadily built on that success . <p> " We 're definitely among the elite on our tiny little team of four athletes . We 've got a world champion , we 've got three World Cup wins , world junior champion , over 20 podiums . This has all happened since Beijing . Before that , none of us had even been in the top 10 . " <p> They wo n't , however , be contesting the mixed team event at the Predazzo Ski Jumping Stadium in Val di Fiemme , amidst the Dolomites , for these Games after not having qualified a second male . <p> One of those World Cup golds was achieved by then 19-year-old Alex Loutitt in 2023 , a first for Canada . But Loutitt suffered a season-ending knee injury during qualification on the large hill at Predazzo in October . Strate describes her closeness with Loutitt as a " sister bond . " She 's missed her deeply : " I do n't even have someone at the top to give high-fives to anymore . " <p> Of course , Loutitt 's absence has cranked up the pressure on Strate for the Olympics . <p> " There 's a lot of pressure on me from all angles , ' ' she admits . " I know what we want to achieve and I want that so bad . I think I 'm handling it pretty well . But the Olympics are a different breed . Then I look at the fact that I 'm the only one from Canada that 's going to be fighting for a medal or even top 10 . <p> " I 'm realistic , knowing that I 've only won one competition this year . But , I have won a competition . Which means I can win another one . <p> " One hundred per cent , that 's what I 'm going for in Italy . " <p> Read more <p> Updated 3 hrs ago <h> Oh , the humanity . Olympic opening ceremony feels both irrelevant and worth the effort <p> Final torchbearer Sofia Goggia. an Italian skier , uses the torch of the Olympic flame to light the Olympic cauldron during the opening ceremony of the 2026 Winter Olympics in in Cortina d'Ampezzo , Italy . <p> Stefano Rellandini/Pool photo via The Associated Press Toronto Star <p> MILAN -- Every opening ceremony has a theme , and a moment , and some are more memorable than others . <p> Sochi 's opening , as a Russian university student told me at the time , was a vision of Russia 's soul without Vladimir Putin . Rio 's included a plea to save the planet . Pyeongchang ended with two young women 's hockey players , one North Korean and one South Korean , climbing a steep set of glowing stairs , carrying one Olympic flame . Beautiful . <p> And Vancouver 2010 is remembered for the broken cauldron , or Wayne Gretzky in the flatbed of a pickup truck with the flame , but more for K.D Lang singing Leonard Cohen 's " Hallelujah , " on the same day that a too-fast luge track killed a young slider from Georgia . That endured for me in the way that song does : not in the words , but in the feeling . <p> An unprecedented four-site , dual-cauldron Winter Olympics opening ceremony replete with references to Italian icons and culture has officially kicked off the Milan Cortina Games , as the sports spectacle returns to a nation that last hosted the event 20 years ago . ( Feb. 6 , 2026 ) <p> AP Video Toronto Star <p> At Milan and Cortina 2026 , Italy went plenty of places . This nation has so many accomplishments in which to luxuriate : architecture and sculpture , opera and art , food and fashion , history and myth . ( The Italians also brought in born-in-America Mariah Carey , the way Paris 2024 imported Canada 's Celine Dion . It 's like an import-a-diva spree . ) <p> Every country has a song it wants to sing to itself and to the world at these things , and it 's what you do with the opening that resonates , or not . Italy decided to emphasize humanity , and more specifically , harmony , or armonia , which in Italian means " bringing together what is different . " Creative director Marco Balich , who also directed the opening in Turin 2006 , had said of the ceremony , " The essence is human-driven , and the emotion comes from humanity . This is our secret ingredient . " <p> Through that lens , the ceremony worked . An actor recited " L'infinito ( The Infinite ) " by Giacomo Leopardi , about contemplating what it means to be human in the universe ; a violin phenom played a 1716 Stradivarius , which soared ; models stalked and dancers danced , and the glorious gibberish of " **25;2824;TOOLONG " was gibberished , and Andrea Bocelli sang " Nessun Dorma , " and hand gestures abounded . That was the theme : it was the stuff of people . <p> The parade of athletes was separated into four locations -- Milan , Cortina , Livigno and Predazzo -- as part of these dispersed Games , spread all over the country , but it was its own tableau . Haiti 's outfits were fantastic , among others . An Iranian female athlete wiped at her eye . Israel was booed , and Ukraine was cheered , and cheered . And while American athletes were cheered , vice-president J.D. Vance was booed until they got him off the big screen . Do you know how hard it is to get booed at the Olympics ? It 's like a Russian told me at the end of the 2014 Games in Sochi : she said , " I do n't cheer for Russia . I cheer for Russians . " It was a moment . <p> And Italy , like every host nation , cheered their hearts out for their own athletes . That 's as it should be . <p> The wild , venal wreckage of America will be a major theme at these Olympics , in a world full of evils . These ceremonies do n't stop anything , and they do n't change anything . They may be watched by hundreds of millions around the world , and they can be soulful pageants that celebrate the best of humans : our art , our sports , our collaboration . <p> But the creative director of the Rio opening in 2016 , Fernando Meirelles , said he wanted to show tolerance was the answer in a world of Donald Trump , of Brexit , and of the political tension in Brazil and the world : The ceremony included maps inspired by NASA climate charts , and a call to save the Amazon . Meirelles said , " We are appealing to the world about this impasse on the planet . The whole planet is under threat . The human race is under threat . So there are strong aspects of this . " <p> Ten years later and here we are , watching another cavalcade of humans trying their best to make something that will make people feel something . And more and more , in a world of hatred and AI inhumanity , an opening ceremony feels both more irrelevant and even more worth the effort . <p> Trying matters . Two days ago , U.K. snowboarder Gus Kenworthy put up an Instagram post which portrayed the words FUCK ICE as if they 'd been peed in the snow , and that got a lot of attention . But Thursday , American snowboarder and two-time gold medallist Chloe Kim put something on Instagram , too . This is her third Games . She said she was grateful . <p> " My parents left South Korea in search of a better future for their family , " Kim wrote . " They left behind everything they knew so that my sisters and I could have the chance to one day live the American dream . Their sacrifice is one I will never take for granted . So when I stand at the top of the halfpipe , competing on the biggest stage in sport , I do so with immense pride . I am representing my country but I am also representing those who took a leap of faith , who came to this beautiful nation carrying hopes , dreams , and courage . <p> " I am proud of my heritage . I am proud of my journey . And I am proud to represent a country that is strongest when it embraces diversity , dignity , and hope . " <p> It wo n't change anything either , but it 's better she said it : It 's better to say the good things , the brave things , the human things , out loud . Olympic openings can be hokey or overlong or incomprehensible without a guide , but at their best , Olympic ceremonies are about how we want to see ourselves , and who we want to be . This is Milan and Cortina and Italy , and the world is welcome . <p> Canadian athletes parade during the opening ceremony of the Milan-Cortina 2026 Winter Olympic Games at the famed soccer cathedral , San Siro stadium , in Milan . <p> Piero Cruciatti/AFP via Getty Images Toronto Star <p> MILAN -- Una bella figura . <p> For those who do n't capisce Italian , that 's an idiom meaning a whole bunch of things wrapped up in a few words : Stylish , cutting a fine figure , making a good impression , impeccable presentation , social grace . <p> Like the elegant carabiniere swishing his long black cloak lined in red silk . <p> Like the majestic and swank spectacle of the opening ceremony on Friday evening at San Siro stadium , a 99-year-old cathedral of Italian football , shared home to AC Milan and Inter Milan , preening in its last extravaganza -- scheduled to be demolished when a replacement stadium is constructed next door , in time for Italy to co-host UEFA Euro 2032 . <p> An unprecedented four-site , dual-cauldron Winter Olympics opening ceremony replete with references to Italian icons and culture has officially kicked off the Milan Cortina Games , as the sports spectacle returns to a nation that last hosted the event 20 years ago . ( Feb. 6 , 2026 ) <p> AP Video Toronto Star <p> The boom is still quite some time coming but the bang reverberated around the amphitheatre , admittedly not a very pretty concrete colossus but steeped in sports history . <p> Big stage on this night for a very big show beheld by the nearly 76,000 in attendance and the multimillions watching on TV around the continents , not the just the 93 countries that have actually sent athletes to these Games , including winter debuts for the decidedly un-ice and un-snow of Benin , Guinea-Bissau and the United Arab Emirates . <p> These dispersed Olympics in Milan-Cortina could create an opportunity for Quebec City to host <p> A co-host , actually , for these bifurcated Olympics , split between Milan ( ice ) and Cortina d'Ampezzo ( snow ) , where the other major opening panorama was held simultaneously -- the " polycentric model " -- as well as smaller satellite trunk shows in Livigno and Predazzo . A never-before version of the Games and , to my mind anyway , hopefully never again , so spread out , sprawling and siloed . Where a snowboarder will never brush up against a short-track skater , nor a luger with a hockey player . <p> It would be impossible for Milan-Cortina to out-spectacle the opening lollapalooza of the Paris Games two years ago , where athletes were borne on boats along the River Seine . But the Winter Olympics are a different creature , with their distinct flair , if maybe lower wattage . Though still all a-sparkle in a gazillion LED lights twinkling throughout the venue as the performance narrative -- its theme " Armonia " ( harmony of beauty and peace ) -- began with a balletic depiction of the myth of Cupid and Psyche , followed by humongous paint tubes settling above the stage . Representing Italian art and design , pride and passion . I think . Followed by a " Symphony of Fantasia , " inspired by the three great masters of Italian opera : Verdi , Puccini and Rossini . <p> Not quite the splashy pop concert of the reunited Spice Girls for London 2012 but Italy swings to its own rhythms . ( Italy 's official song for these Olympics is the 1977 smash hit " Ti Amo . " ) <p> Into this tableau , in a cloud of feathers and gossamer -- designed by Fausto Puglisi -- stepped Mariah Carey , whose connection to Italy is beyond my grasp , but she performed " Nel blu , dipinto di blu " ( sounded like " Volare " to me ) and her big hit from a lifetime ago , " Nothing Is Impossible . " <p> With musical accompaniment -- from the full orchestra to the soloist playing a precious 1716 Stradivarius violin -- the show raced through a kaleidoscope of Italian history and culture , everything from couture to cuisine , after which a covey of high-fashion models dressed in Armani brought out a giant flag to be hoisted up the pole , while the president of the republic and IOC president Kirsty Coventry took their seats . And the Olympics rings exploded in pyrotechnics . <p> International Olympic Committee President Kirsty Coventry received the Olympic flame from American Olympic rower Anita DeFrantz in Milan on Friday ahead of the opening ceremony . ( Feb. 6 , 2026 ) <p> AP Video Toronto Star <p> The storytellers left out , however , that whole Fascist bit . It should be noted though that Il Duce , after he was executed by firing squad on April 28 , 1945 , in the final days of the Second World War , had his body transported to Milan , hung upside down ( ditto his mistress , Clara Petacci ) from a metal girder at Piazzale Loreto . <p> What everybody wanted most to see , of course , was the parade of nations , athletes entering the stadium and promenading around the swirling lanes of the stage . Led , as always , by Team Greece . While , I swear to God , music was played by a DJ dressed like the Pope . <p> Which was kind of funny because organizers had gone out of their way to emphasize that this opening ceremony was intended to unify and not provoke , which I took as a shot against the controversial drag queen performers in Paris . <p> Because these Games are chopped up in different clusters , the athletic delegations were considerably smaller than customary , each nation falling in behind snow queens carrying placards identifying the countries , wrapped in silver puffer coats with fanning trains . Sometimes , because all the athletes were in other villages , the snow queens were followed by nobody . <p> Quite a few athletes , those with imminent competition , chose not to participate , prioritizing rest and sleep . But Team Canada strutted in considerable numbers , about 70 of them , and received a roaring welcome from the crowd . Because we are an admired country , with nice people , except maybe not the people dossing in my doorway . <p> Two aerial actors perform next to the rings representing the city and mountain . <p> Matthias Hangst/Getty Images Toronto Star <p> Although that Lululemon kit of burgundy ( looked like mudpie brown ) and bright red accents did n't really do much for the athletes visually , certainly not in a city that knows from fashion . But what do I know about vogue ? I 'm wearing a Cruella de Vil coat made of rabbit fur . And I have n't met a Canadian athlete who dislikes the unis . <p> Not included in the tally of nations were Russia and Belarus , both still Olympic pariahs over the unprovoked invasion of Ukraine , though Russia has a small contingent of athletes here as " individual neutral athletes , " their flag and anthem forbidden . <p> Team Israel got booed . Which you might think is a reaction to the ruinous war in Gaza but I 've heard Israel booed at every Olympics I 've covered since at least the turn of the millennium . And it took the IOC a mere 44 years to officially commemorate the 11 Israeli athletes and coaches who were slaughtered by Black September at the 1972 Munich Olympics . <p> Plenty of jeers for Team USA , too , although that surely had less to do with Americans than you-know-who . <p> Wild reception , naturally , for Team Italy , while the orchestra played a lively tarantella . " <h> This Canadian is the master icemaker at the Olympics <p> There 's been a good deal of concern at the 2026 Winter Olympics about hockey and curling ice but today the spotlight is on the long-track speedskating ice where Canada is looking to win its first Olympic medal . <p> This is the first Olympics to use a temporary building for long-track speedskating and that creates a slew of new challenges ( on top of the all the regular ones ) to create and maintain the perfect ice skaters rely on . That job falls to Calgary 's Mark Messer , the master icemaker who has been in charge of oval ice for seven Olympics . <p> He says weather , especially if it 's raining or humid , is a challenge and even the heat load from fans cheering , say , if the Italians are winning a race , versus sitting quietly makes a difference . The floor structure in the building , made from two temporarily joined exhibition halls , means extra work to balance ice that 's thin enough to achieve the correct temperature for racing and thick enough that the six-tonne Zamboni does n't break it . <p> Snowboard cross athlete Tess Critchlow withdrew prior to the Games due to injury , while three-time medallist Mark McMorris missed the men 's snowboard big air qualification after a crash in training , though he hopes to return for slopestyle . <p> O'Dine said she had previously fractured her ankle in November , and expressed pride on how she 'd managed to return to form . <p> " All you can do is look forward and take the best care of yourself , " she said . <p> When Olympian Adam Runnalls puts down his rifle , he picks up knitting needles . <p> The Canadian biathlete prepared for his second Olympics by crafting hats and sweaters after his coach suggested he take up knitting in December . The 27-year-old Olympian says he 's found a hidden crossover between sports fans and knitters . <p> " There 's a lot of people who like sports and like knitting , " the Calgary native says . " It 's really positive to have male representation in the knitting world and men doing it , especially at the Olympics , or high-level athletes , people enjoy seeing that representation in the community . " <p> Runnalls started on a sweater at Olympic pre-camp and says his goal is to finish by the time he leaves Italy . The biathlon schedule starts Sunday for the women . Runnalls ' first event is the individual 20 kilometres on Tuesday . <p> Biathlon requires athletes to calm their minds in an instant while they aim at a target with hearts racing after cross-country skiing . Training keeps him pretty busy , so he works away at the yarn while on a stationary bike . Runnalls says knitting helps him relax while still using his hands . <p> " I 'm always moving , so the knitting allows me to sit and be focused on something . " <p> He is the only member of Canada 's eight-member biathlon team with any Olympic experience . She has had stronger results this season in the large hill event , which comes later in the Games . <p> Updated 1 hr ago <h> This Canadian figure skater asked for an extension on a school assignment . She 's busy competing at the Olympics <p> Schizas had gone viral after declaring any other Canadian woman would make it to Milan " over my dead body . " And she did n't take it back after <p> The fiery figure skater went viral in January when she declared that another skater would go to the Olympics , " over my dead body , " after challenger Gabrielle Daleman almost made a comeback in the Canadian figure skating championships in Gatineau , Que . <p> Ca n't tell a melon from a method grab ? No worries . Here 's a cheat sheet of some terms you might hear when the action starts in Livigno , Italy . <p> The main events <p> Halfpipe : Imagine a massive tunnel that you could drive a truck through , slice the top off , then transform it into hard-packed snow . That is the halfpipe where snowboarders and freeskiers take turns zooming across its inclined slope to gather speed before launching into a series of acrobatic jumps . The halfpipe at the Livigno ski resort hosting these Olympic events measures 220 meters ( 240 yards ) in length , 22 meters ( 24 yards ) in width , and rises to 7.2 meters ( 23.6 feet ) in height . <p> Slopestyle : A course located on a slope where snowboarders and freestyle skiers take turns trying to put together the most polished and demanding series of tricks coming off rails and molded bumps for aerial lift . The Livigno slopestyle course allows for jumps that can exceed 25 meters ( 27 yards ) . <p> Big air : The third of the " park " events consists of athletes performing one single " big " jump . The Livigno big air jump ramp peaks at over 40 meters ( yards ) . <p> The tricky details <p> 180 , 360 , 540 , 1440 : The degrees of a spinning trick completed in a single jump . 360 is 1 complete spin , 720 two spins , etc . The most spins completed in competition are 6 1/2 . That 's a 2340 , both on snowboard and skis , and the massive spins like that almost always come in big air . On the halfpipe , anyone who pulls off a 1440 will be in contention for a medal . Important for street credibility : 2340 and the like are pronounced " twenty-three forty , " not " two thousand , three-hundred and forty . " <p> Backside : Facing backwards going into a rail on a slopestyle course , as well as when a rider or skier faces uphill when completing a spin on a jump . Frontside means a rider faces downhill when completing a spin . <p> Cork : A head-over-heels flip that produces a corkscrew effect . A double cork is two flips , a triple cork , three , etc . Triple corks are the trick that could win the men 's halfpipe . <p> Grabs : How the snowboarder reaches down and holds the board during a jump . There are many , but among those that come up for the top riders : Mute ( front hand grabs the toe edge ) , melon ( front hand grabs the back edge ) , stalefish ( grab heel edge with rear hand ) . method ( a melon but with a little flair , tweaking the board up and twisting ) and , of course , the crail ( both hands and back leg should be straight ; do not try this at home ) . Judges look to see whether the rider actually grabs the board , not just places their hand on top of it , and some of these grabs need to be executed between bindings . <p> Grabs , Part 2 : Freeskiing has its own glossary of these grabs , including Japan ( behind and across to grab opposite ski ) and seat belt ( imagine putting a seatbelt on , but by bringing the buckle up to grab the latch ) . <p> Goofy : When a snowboarder leads with his " bad foot . " For a right-handed person , it would mean leading with the right foot . <p> Tomahawk : A steak Shaun White ate at the X Games in Aspen that doubles as his preferred name for what 's more commonly known as the Double McTwist 1260 , a trick with 3 1/2 spins ( two of them head over heels ) that starts with a backside spin and is still considered one of the toughest out there . <p> Cab : A basic of most halfpipe rides , it 's named after a skateboarder , Steve Caballero . It starts with a switch approach into a frontside spin . <p> Yolo : " You Only Live Once . " This is a Cab , double cork 1440 that was the focal point of the 2014 Olympics . White saw Swiss rider Iouri " iPod " Podladtchikov land it in the lead-up , then went about trying to perfect it himself ; it was the iPod who pulled it off at the Olympics to win gold . The Yolo is still a relevant trick but in a sign of how much things have changed , it 's now a triple cork ( or two ) -- not on the board eight years ago -- that will likely win this year 's Olympics . <p> MILAN ( AP ) -- Italian police fired tear gas and a water cannon at dozens of protesters who threw firecrackers and tried to access a highway near a Winter Olympics venue on Saturday . <p> The brief confrontation came at the end of a peaceful march by thousands against the environmental impact of the Games and the presence of U.S. agents in Italy . <p> Police held off the violent demonstrators , who appeared to be trying to reach the Santagiulia Olympic ice hockey rink , after the skirmish . @@134302301 <h> How to survive polycrisis fatigue and focus your compassion without burning out <h> Key points <p> Polycrisis fatigue drains your nervous system . <p> Empathy has limits , and it 's important to learn to pause without guilt . <p> Small , consistent actions count . <p> When I 'm in the kitchen cooking , I like to watch the news as I chop and stir . But lately , the routine has turned corrosive . The reports do n't stay in the background ; they invade the foreground of my mind . <p> A bulletin on the war in Ukraine , with footage of shattered lives . An update on the grinding conflict in Gaza . Shootings of citizens in Minnesota . Before I can process one tragedy , an ASPCA commercial shows the pleading eyes of a starving dog , triggering another wave of helpless sorrow . <p> Ukraine War <p> Source : Palinchuk/Dreamstime <p> I stand at the counter , feeling my breath shorten . It 's not just sadness . It 's a physiological siege , a constant assault on my nervous system . If @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ looking away . If I leave it on , I feel myself crumbling under the weight of a world in pain . <p> This is the modern dilemma of compassion : We are connected to a global tapestry of suffering , yet neurologically unequipped to hold it all . Systems theorists describe this convergence of overlapping global stressors as a polycrisis , a term popularized by Edgar Morin to describe crises that compound rather than resolve independently . <h> The Science of the Siege <p> Human brains evolved to respond to acute , local threats . A predator appears , the amygdala sounds the alarm , stresshormones are released , we fight or flee , and then the threat passes . Recovery begins . <p> The kitchen TV delivers a fundamentally different kind of threat : chronic , global , and unresolvable . It activates the same ancient alarm system -- the amygdala , the cortisol rush -- but offers no tangible predator to confront and no safe place to flee . Neuroendocrinologist Bruce McEwen described the result as allostatic load : the cumulative wear and tear on the body @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> This kind of exposure does n't need to be firsthand to be harmful . Research shows that after events such as the Boston Marathon bombing , repeated media exposure predicted acute stress symptoms equal to or greater than direct exposure . The body responds as if danger is ongoing , even when the threat is distant and abstract . <p> Our compassion , too , has limits . Psychologist Paul Slovic 's work reveals a painful paradox : We are deeply moved by a single , identifiable victim , but as the number of victims grows , our empathy often collapses into helplessness , a phenomenon he calls psychic numbing . <p> The modern news cycle , with its constant bombardment of tragic clips from across the globe , forces our empathy circuits to toggle at an impossible speed . System failure becomes inevitable . <h> From Firefighter to Gardener <p> When faced with a field on fire , our instinct is to become firefighters -- to rush toward every blaze with our small bucket . This is a guaranteed path to burnout . The alternative is to become @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ water every parched field on earth . Instead , they tend to specific , chosen plots . Life is sustained not by frantic , scattered effort , but by consistent , focused cultivation . <p> This is the mindset we need for our most precious resources : attention and compassion . Finite resources , like water contained in a rain barrel . <p> The news is a storm . Stand in the deluge and you drown . To survive , you collect and distribute the water intentionally . <p> Garden Plots <p> Source : Osu855/Dreamstime <h> Step 1 : Take Pauses Without Guilt <p> When the deluge begins , change the channel . Turn from news to music , a podcast , or silence . <p> Choose one or two causes for this season of your life and go deep , not wide . Focused learning and sustained giving live here . Research on self-efficacy shows that achievable , focused action restores a sense of agency ; this is one of the strongest buffers against helplessness and burnout . <p> Leave room for spontaneous care : a fundraiser , a @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ not obligation . <h> Step 3 : Compost the Guilt <p> The feeling that you should be doing more is the toxic runoff of constant exposure to suffering . Psychologists describe this tension as moral distress , a concept originally studied in healthcare workers and increasingly applied to civilian populations living amid chronic crises . <p> " I am one person with one watering can . I can not stop all fires , but I can grow life where I am . " <h> The Peaceful Kitchen <p> Now , when I 'm in the kitchen , I often cook listening to a single , in-depth podcast on a topic I 've chosen to follow . My phone is no longer a live wire of alerts . I 've built my rain barrel . <p> A rain barrel does n't stop the rain . It prevents flooding by collecting what falls and releasing it slowly , where it can actually nourish something . Strategic compassion works the same way . You do n't absorb every tragedy in real time ; you contain it , regulate your nervous system , and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> I am not disengaged from the world . I am engaging with it on terms my nervous system can sustain . <p> The goal is to become the kind of people who remain standing when the smoke clears . Not burnt out , but resilient . <p> We can not save the whole burning field . But we can each learn to garden . And a world full of gardeners , tending their chosen plots with patience and care , might just grow a forest capable of withstanding the fire .

@@134339476 <h> The Money Line Dividing The Democratic Party <p> WASHINGTON ? The Democratic Party is in the middle of fierce intraparty debates over everything from health care to immigration enforcement to the War in Gaza , but the issue which could drive more primary fights than any other has as much to do with how candidates fill their campaign coffers as it does with the policy positions they hold . <p> Divisions between candidates over whether to take corporate PAC money are erupting in at least four major Democratic Senate primaries this year ? in Michigan , Minnesota , Texas and Illinois ? and in a host of fights for U.S. House nominations . Candidates turning down the cash , typically but not always progressives , are arguing their opponents ' ability to stand up to President Donald Trump and fight special interests is inherently crippled by their decision to take the donations . <p> Advertisement <p> " If you take money from certain people , it 's not like that money does n't come with strings , " said Abdul El-Sayed , a progressive who is running for Michigan @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ " I do n't have any strings attached to any corporations who stand to gain based on what I say or what I do . " <p> Those who take the money , however , are working to muddy the waters by pointing to other ways their opponents have benefited from corporate money , painting their opponents as holier-than-thou . <p> The fights have turned what was once a uniting issue for Democrats ? progressives and moderates alike turned down corporate PAC money during the 2018 blue wave midterm ? into another intraparty pressure point , one which progressives have been happy to emphasize . They argue the issue is more important than ever , especially with the tech and other powerful industries going all-in to win Trump 's approval . <p> Advertisement <p> " People are fed up with a system that 's rigged in favor of price-gouging corporations and understand the corrupting influence of money in politics is a key factor why politicians have failed to lower costs like health care and housing , " said Sen. Elizabeth Warren ( D-Mass . ) . " Rejecting corporate PAC @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 're a Democrat with backbone who will actually fight back against special interests . " <p> Warren and other progressives have taken to pointing to rejecting corporate PAC money as a significant litmus test . Sen. Chris Murphy ( D-Conn. ) , another member of the so-called " Fight Club " of liberal senators specifically pointed to it as a reason for endorsing Lt. Gov . Peggy Flanagan 's Senate bid in Minnesota and progressive Maine Senate candidate Graham Platner . <p> Direct corporate donations to candidates are illegal under federal law , though corporations can donate to both super PACs and to nonprofits which engage in political activity . Corporate PACs , which are often controlled by a company or trade association employee , typically accept donations from executives and shareholders in a company and then distribute the cash to favored candidates . <p> Advertisement <p> Corporate PAC money makes up somewhere around 5% of the cash donated to federal campaigns each cycle , but turning it down has become a major symbolic measure for many Democrats and a handful of Republicans who argue it 's representative of a @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ typical voters . Some candidates who turn it down are unlikely to receive much of it in the first place . <p> Michigan is perhaps the state where the divides are sharpest , as shown by the arguments at a candidate forum hosted by the United Auto Workers on Wednesday . After a UAW member directly asked if the candidates were taking corporate PAC money , both El-Sayed and state Sen. Mallory McMorrow said they were n't . <p> Rep. Haley Stevens , the most moderate of the three candidates and the preferred candidate of Democratic leaders in Washington , does not apologize for her willingness to take corporate PAC donations . She instead focused on her desire to overturn the Supreme Court 's Citizens United decision . <p> Advertisement <p> " I 'm not a millionaire , and I do n't own stock , " Stevens said . " I 'm running my campaign in a grassroots way .
